Its settings turn on UX 2.0 and leave off what would need a model or a connector (the Slack look, the standup, meeting and todo notes, the diff's story and questions) and the update check; since its services are on this computer, it looks often (`poll_fast`) and doesn't pause its lookups while no terminal is attached (`pause_when_away`).

## Your own files

Everything of your own is in `~/.config/switchboard/`, which nothing writes over but Settings, so an update leaves it be. Each file is read after Switchboard's defaults, so what it says wins, and Settings lists each setting in these files under the file's name.

### Your files in a git repository

To have the same files on every computer, keep them in a git repository of yours, a dotfiles repository say. Name it in `~/.config/switchboard/files.json` (`{"repo": "<its URL, as git clone takes it>", "dir": "switchboard"}`, or Settings' "Your files' repository" rows; `dir` is the folder in it that holds your files, `switchboard` when not set and `.` for its root) and turn on Settings' "Your files from a git repository" (`files_from_repo`, off by default, since it sends your settings to a remote). The server then clones it into `~/.local/share/switchboard/files`, copies into its folder the files you have in `~/.config/switchboard` that it lacks (where both have a file the repository's wins, and yours stays where it was), and from then on every file of your own is read from and written to `~/.local/share/switchboard/files/<dir>`: Settings, an edit by hand and `switchboard setting` all write there.

It pulls the repository as the server starts and every ten minutes, fast-forward only, and within a couple of seconds of a change to a file there commits it ("Switchboard: settings.json") and pushes it, with git's own hooks, your identity and, on GitHub, gh's sign-in among the credential helpers; it never prompts. Before a push, `switchboard leak-check` reads what it would publish, and anything it finds is asked about first in a box listing it: Enter pushes, and No leaves the commits in the clone, unpushed, asked about again only once there is more to push. A push that fails (no network) is said once and tried again with the next change or pull. When the repository and this computer have both changed, so the remote can't be taken fast-forward, nothing is merged or pushed and a message says so: bring the clone up to date by hand (`git pull --rebase` in it) and the next pull goes on. `M-p`'s "Sync your Switchboard files now" (`sync-your-files`) pulls and pushes at once and says how it went.

Some files never leave the computer, read and written in `~/.config/switchboard` whatever `files.json` says: `files.json` itself, with the setting that turns this on (kept in the `settings.json` there); what decides the code that runs here with no one asked, so a pull never does (`trusted-dirs`, `repos.json`, whose commands a landing runs, and `sidebar-plugins/`, whose programs the server runs); `slack-relay.json`, which names the one person the relay answers to; and `secrets.json`, `linear.json` (it may hold your Linear key) and `leak-check.txt`, which hold what must not be published; and the files Claude's skills read by their path (`oncall.json`, `oncall-rules.md`, `slack-updates.json`). So are the `.bak` copies. The clone checks out no symbolic links, so a link kept in the repository arrives as a plain file naming its target and can't lead a write out of the clone. What does move is `settings.json`, `keys.json`, `sidebar.json`, `browser.json`, `claude.json` and `CLAUDE.md`; whenever where they are changes, `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` is written again to import your rules from there. A pull does bring your settings and where your CLAUDE.md's rules come from (`claude.json`), so name only a repository you trust with those, one only you can push to. When your CLAUDE.md's rules come from the same repository (`claude.json`'s `repo`), one clone serves both. With the setting off, or `files.json` gone, your files are the ones in `~/.config/switchboard` again, as they were when the repository was named; the clone is left where it is. `switchboard try` copies your files from the clone without `files.json`, so a try reads its own copy and pushes nothing.

| File | What it holds | Where it is described |
|---|---|---|
| `settings.json` | What you changed in Settings, only the settings that differ from their defaults; you may edit it by hand | "Every setting", below |
| `keys.json` | Keys bound to other actions, or unbound | "Changing the keys" |
| `sidebar.json`, `sidebar-plugins/` | The sidebar's and mission control's sizes, and the plugins that load | "sidebar.json", "Plugins" |
| `browser.json` | The Chrome profile each site's links open in, and the default one | "Where links open" |
| `linear.json` | Your Linear workspace, and the team and labels `/shape` files tickets with | "GitHub and Linear access" |
| `CLAUDE.md`, `claude.json` | Your own Claude rules, or where they come from: another file or a git repository | "Your CLAUDE.md" |
| `slack-updates.json` | The Slack channels the Slack view shows in full | "The Slack view" |
| `slack-relay.json` | Your Slack member ID, the one person the relay listens to | "The Slack app, and keeping it yours" |
| `oncall.json`, `oncall-rules.md` | The channels `/oncall` watches, and your team's rules | "On call: `/oncall`" |
| `repos.json` | Each repository's own settings by its origin's `owner/name`: how its work lands, through a PR or straight on its default branch, a direct landing's tests and where they run (here or in CI), squash, review and command before the push, and the `apply` list of what a change landed there needs to take effect | "Landing: `/queue-and-clean`" |
| `leak-check.txt` | Your organization's and company's names, usernames and internal hosts, one a line (blank lines and `#` lines left out), which `switchboard leak-check` finds, as whole words in any case, in what a landing would push to a public repository | "Landing: `/queue-and-clean`" |
| `files.json` | The git repository your files are kept in (`repo`) and the folder in it that holds them (`dir`), read while Settings' "Your files from a git repository" is on; it stays on the computer | "Your files in a git repository" |
| `trusted-dirs` | The directories whose own code may run, one a line: `switchboard try` builds a worktree holding someone else's commits only once it is listed, and `/queue-and-clean` offers to run a landed repository's install or migrate script only in one listed; a PR checked out untrusted stays untrusted when listed | "Trying a change" |
| `secrets.json` | On Linux with no keyring, the secrets `switchboard platform secret-set` stores, readable only by you | "GitHub and Linear access" |

What Switchboard keeps for itself is in `~/.local/state/switchboard/` (the socket, the saved layout, the tips shown, what it counted, the day's files) and its caches in `~/.cache/switchboard/` and `~/.cache/wt-revive/` (what each Claude transcript says of its worktree, for `wt-revive`); the Slack relay's state is in `~/.local/state/slack-relay/`, and `/oncall`'s in `~/.local/state/oncall/`. `~/.local/share/switchboard/` holds what setup writes for Claude Code: Switchboard's plugin (`claude/plugin/`, where earlier builds' `plugin/` is moved and left as a link to it for the tabs opened before, with `.switchboard-plugin` listing what was written, and what a skill installed for itself beside its scripts), Switchboard's part of your CLAUDE.md when that imports it (`switchboard.md`), the clone of your rules' repository (`claude-md/`) and the build whose parts were last put in place (`setup-build`). None of it is yours to edit, and any of it can be deleted while the server is stopped, at the cost of what it held.

## Every setting

Mission control's Settings view (`M-p`, then "Settings: turn the workspace's options on and off") lists every option below under its category; Enter or Space twice flips an on/off one, an option of choices listing every choice in its menu, and `/` filters them. A new feature comes with a setting, on unless it sends something of yours somewhere, so each part can be turned off for a plain workspace. `switchboard setting <key>` prints one's value, `switchboard setting <key> <value>` changes it (`on` or `off`, or one of its choices, or for `lone_pane_width` a number from 80, or for `new_tab_home` a folder, `~/...` or absolute, that is there; anything else is refused), and `--reset` gives it its default back.

### Integrations

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| GitHub (`github`) | on | PR commands in M-p, the PRs view, PR numbers and merge states in tabs and Reopen's worktrees; off, the workspace asks GitHub nothing by itself (programs you run, such as codex-review and wtpr, still do) |
| Linear (`linear`) | on | Ticket commands in M-p, ticket titles in tab names and tickets in M-j's picker; off, the workspace asks Linear nothing by itself (programs you run, such as wt and worktree-done, still do) |
| Cache PR and ticket images (`image_cache`) | on | Keeps the pictures, stylesheet, scripts and drawn pages of the PR and ticket image views on this computer, up to 300 MB, so one opened again shows at once; off, everything is fetched and drawn each time, and the pictures are left out, since the page loads a picture only from a copy on this computer |
| Docs in the link view (`link_view_docs`) | on | The link view reads a Notion page or a Google Doc or Slides deck through Claude's connectors, a headless Haiku fetching it; off, they are read as any other page, which a private one won't let it |
| Chrome profiles (`chrome_profiles`) | on | Links Switchboard opens (mission control, the link view, M-r, M-l, switchboard open-url) open in the Chrome profile browser.json gives their site, else its default profile; off, every link opens in the default browser |
| Standup note (`standup_note`) | on | Each weekday from 6am, a note in Standups written from the last working day: the PRs you merged, opened, worked on and reviewed, your tickets that changed state and your commits, then what is in progress today and the reviews waiting on you |
| Standup summary (`standup_summary`) | on | Over the standup note's list of the last working day, two or three sentences summing it up, written by Claude (Haiku, no tools) from the PR, ticket and commit titles in that list |
| Today's todo note (`todo_note`) | off | Whether the Today's todo note starter routine is made running when this version first sets up the routines (it sends your notes' unticked items to Claude); after that, pause or resume it in the Routines view |
| Todo note time (`todo_note_time`) | 9am | The hour the Today's todo note starter routine runs at each day, when this version first sets up the routines; after that, change its schedule in the Routines view |
| Meeting notes (`meeting_notes`) | on | Whether the Meeting notes starter routine is made running when this version first sets up the routines (once Fathom has worked); after that, pause or resume it in the Routines view |
| Search everything (`search_everything`) | on | M-S-f opens a box that searches, with a chip to turn each source on or off, and lists the open tabs that name or show it and what your notes, Linear, GitHub and Slack hold for it, each read beside the pane with Enter, a tab gone to |
| Arrow keys reach the search chips (`search_arrows`) | on | In M-S-f's box, Down goes from the field to the chips and on to the results, Up back the same way, and Left and Right move along the chips; off, Up and Down move through the results alone and Tab reaches the chips |
| Preview the chosen search result (`search_preview`) | on | M-S-f's box is wider and shows the chosen result beside the results (under them in a narrow box): where it was found and the lines around the match, the match's line lit; J and K scroll it with the results focused; off, the box lists the results alone |
| Slack in search everything (`search_slack`) | on | M-S-f searches Slack too, through your own Slack sign-in while "Read Slack through your own token" is on, else through Claude's connector, a headless Haiku making that one call, its results arriving a few seconds after the others |
| Search tabs' conversations (`tab_search_conversation`) | on | Search everything (M-S-f) finds a tab by what was said in its Claude conversation as well, your prompts and Claude's replies (not its tool calls), read from the session's transcript where Claude Code wrote it on this computer and kept in memory, never sent anywhere; Enter on a tab found that way opens its history (M-h) at the moment it was said. Off, search everything finds tabs by their names and what their panes show alone |

### Notifications

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Push notifications (`notifications`) | on | A desktop notification, named for the tab, when a Claude tab waits on you |
| Finished tabs clear (`done_clears`) | when seen | When a tab's finished mark goes: once you look at the tab, or only when you next prompt it |

### Sidebar

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Sidebar (`sidebar`) | on | The sidebar on the left of every window; M-s still hides or shows it for now |
| Shade the sidebar (`sidebar_panel`) | on | The sidebar is drawn on a shade of its own, a step darker than the panes, as UX 2.0's canvas draws it; off, it is drawn on the terminal's background as before. The chosen row keeps its shade and accent bar either way |
| Half-page scrolling (`sidebar_half_page`) | on | C-d and C-u in the sidebar, and the wheel over it, scroll its list half a screen down or up, the cursor moving with it, as in Vim, one flick of the wheel moving once; off, C-d and C-u run in the chosen tab as other Ctrl keys do and the wheel moves a tab at a time |
| Sidebar repository filter (`sidebar_repo_filter`) | on | M-p's Filter the sidebar to a repository moves every other repository's tabs to an Other repos group at the bottom, whatever they are doing, until Show every repository's tabs clears it; the filter is kept across restarts. Off, the commands and M-R are gone and every tab is in its own group |
| Select several tabs (`sidebar_select`) | on | v in the sidebar, or a Shift-click on a tab, selects several tabs to close at once from one menu. Off, v does nothing, a Shift-click on a tab goes there as a click does, and the sidebar acts on one tab at a time |
| Reminders in the sidebar (`sidebar_reminders`) | on | Tabs waiting on a reminder sit under Reminders in the sidebar. Off, they are left out of the sidebar, the top line counts them (⏰ 3) and a click there opens mission control's Reminders view; a tab comes back under Needs you when its reminder comes due |
| Build failing (`build_failing`) | on | A Build failing group in the sidebar and mission control for the tabs whose open PR has a failing check, asked of GitHub every minute, an idle one wearing a red ✗; off, they stay in Working or Idle with the failing count in their words #github |
| Show the landing queue (`landing_queue`) | on | Tabs landing on the default branch with queue-and-clean, or waiting their turn, get a Landing group in the sidebar and mission control, in the landing queue's order, which a waiting tab's menu changes; off, they show as Working, and the queue still orders the landings |
| Notifications (`sidebar_notifications`) | on | The Notifications block at the bottom of the sidebar: new Slack messages and your Linear inbox, which a click opens in mission control |
| PR notifications (`pr_notices`) | on | The sidebar tells you when one of your PRs merges, is approved or its checks start failing, from the PRs board's lookups; off, it doesn't |
| M-a goes to notifications (`needs_notices`) | on | M-a's walk of what needs you ends at the notifications while there are any, after the tabs, opening them as the sidebar's n does |
| M-a visits Build failing tabs (`walk_build_failing`) | on | M-a's walk of what needs you goes on to the tabs under Build failing after those under Needs you, before the notifications; off, it passes them by |
| Notifications window (`notice_list`) | on | Moving the sidebar's cursor onto a notification, or clicking one, brings the Notifications window to the front as moving onto a tab does: a tab of its own listing every one with its whole message, Enter opening its menu and Esc going back, as M-p's "Go to the notifications" does too; off, Enter or a click on one in the sidebar does what it leads to |
| New notes (`new_notes`) | on | In the sidebar's notifications, a note written for you (a meeting's from Fathom, the standup) until you read it, Enter offering to open it in Notes |
| Next meeting (`next_meeting`) | on | In the sidebar's notifications, from five minutes before a meeting until ten after it starts: its name, the tickets and PRs it mentions, and Enter or a click to join its call |
| Calendar changes (`calendar_changes`) | on | In the sidebar's notifications, what changed on today's calendar since you last looked at it: an event added, moved, renamed or gone; looking at the Calendar view clears them |

### Sessions

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Target branches follow the default branch (`target_follows_default`) | on | When a landing pushes to the default branch while autopilot or hand-started items land on another branch, opens a tab in the background that merges the default branch into that branch under its landing lock, runs the repository's tests and pushes it, so the branch and the builds made from it carry the default branch's work; a conflict or failing tests leave the merge in its worktree for you, marked red in the Landing view. Off, a target branch takes the default branch's work only when you merge it yourself; M-p's 'Bring a target branch up to date with the default branch' still runs it by hand |
| Watch the default branch's CI (`main_ci_watch`) | on | While autopilot works on a repository, or a tab waits to land on it, and its landings test in CI, Switchboard looks at its default branch's CI every two minutes and tells you when it fails, naming the commit and the line of the log that says why. Off, nothing looks, and you hear of a failing default branch only from the next landing |
| Remove landed worktrees (`sweep_landed_worktrees`) | on | Removes a worktree under a repository's .claude/worktrees once everything on its branch is on the default branch, nothing in it is uncommitted, nothing git ignores in it is of its own (tool output and caches, links and copies of the main clone's files go with it), no tab has it open and git hasn't changed its index or HEAD for an hour (a commit, a checkout, a staged file; a read such as git log doesn't count): when a tab in it closes, and hourly for each repository a tab has been in, after fetching the default branch. Its branch is kept, and a message names what went. Off, worktrees stay until you remove them, as Reopen's menu still does |
| Keep the computer awake while tabs work (`keep_awake`) | on | While a tab's Claude works (background work and watches too, not a /loop between its runs) or autopilot, on and not paused, has tabs open, the computer doesn't sleep when idle (caffeinate on macOS, a logind inhibitor on Linux), shown by a ☕ on the status line, or with UX 2.0 at the top of the sidebar; it lets go two minutes after everything is idle, the display still sleeps, and a laptop closed with no display on it sleeps anyway. M-p's 'Keep the computer awake until I say' holds it whatever runs. Off, the computer sleeps on its own schedule unless held from M-p |
| wtpr asks where the PR stands (`wtpr_prompt`) | on | Claude, started in a PR's worktree by wtpr (or mission control's "work on it in a new tab"), first reads the PR, its review threads, failing checks and ticket and says where it stands and what it would do next, changing nothing until you say; off, it starts with no prompt |
| Copy Claude's reply (`copy_reply`) | on | M-y copies the latest reply of the tab's Claude to the clipboard, read from its session transcript; off, the key reaches the pane |
| Programs in panes copy (`pane_clipboard`) | on | A program in the pane in front of you can put text on the clipboard (OSC 52), as Claude Code's /copy and Neovim do; one in a pane you aren't looking at never can. Off, every pane's copy is ignored, and only Switchboard's own copies (copy mode, M-y) reach the clipboard |
| Log Claude's hooks (`hook_log`) | off | Write a line to the server's log (server.log in Switchboard's state directory) for each hook Claude Code runs in a pane: the event, the tool, the pane, the call's id, the subagent and a notification's kind, never what was asked or run; for finding out why a tab showed the wrong state. Off, nothing is logged |
| Offer to apply a landed change (`apply_after_landing`) | on | After queue-and-clean lands work, it lists what the change needs to take effect (Switchboard built and put in place, the Slack relay restarted, an install, a migration) and offers to do it; a tab in autopilot's hands-free mode applies none of it and says so in its landing's notification |
| Save what panes show (`save_contents`) | on | Keep each pane's screen in the layout saves, to show again after a restart, and a closed tab's last screen for the Reopen view; it can hold secrets |
| Restore on start (`auto_restore`) | on | Bring back the last saved tabs and panes, each Claude resumed, when the server starts with none open |
| Load Switchboard as a plugin (`claude_plugin`) | on | Claude in Switchboard's panes loads its hooks (the tabs' Claude states), skills and instructions as a plugin, and setup keeps them out of ~/.claude, so Claude elsewhere has only your own; it needs Claude Code 2.1.280 or later, and managed settings that don't set disableSideloadFlags; a managed plugin that answers Claude Code's settings before Switchboard's leaves its panes without Switchboard's statusline, which Switchboard says when it happens. Off, setup puts them in ~/.claude as before, for every Claude of yours. Changing it moves them over |
| Context in Claude's status line (`statusline_context`) | on | Claude Code's status line under its prompt says how full the context is, beside the model, inside the workspace as outside it. Off, with UX 2.0 and mission control's Usage view on, the status line inside the workspace shows the model alone and the Usage view's Sessions show the context; without both of those it shows the context anyway |
| Switchboard's statusline in its panes (`pane_statusline`) | on | With Switchboard loaded as a plugin, Claude in its panes draws Switchboard's statusline, which keeps each session's context and spend for mission control's Usage view, and your own statusline is drawn everywhere else; nothing is written to your Claude Code settings either way. Off, the plugin leaves the statusline alone, so a Claude started in a pane after the change draws yours too, and that view gets nothing from it |
| Switchboard's permissions in its panes (`pane_permissions`) | on | With Switchboard loaded as a plugin, Claude in its panes runs the few of Switchboard's own commands its instructions name (switchboard tabs, show, set and the like) without asking, unless a rule of yours says otherwise, and is refused reading .env files and running git pull; nothing is written to your Claude Code settings either way. Its skills allow their own commands themselves, whatever this says. Off, a Claude started in a pane after the change asks for those commands as for any other, and refuses nothing for Switchboard |
| Queue heavy work (`slot_queue`) | on | cargo, npm and pnpm run in a pane put builds, test runs and package installs through a queue, so only as many of each kind go at once as the caps below say, and the cores are shared out between them (CARGO_BUILD_JOBS, RUST_TEST_THREADS); a command waiting says "Waiting for a build slot (2nd in line)", and a slot is let go as its command ends or is killed. Off, every command runs at once, as the real program would |
| Builds at once (`slots_build`) | auto | How many compiles (cargo build, check, clippy, doc, and the compile half of cargo test) go at once across every pane while Queue heavy work is on; auto is a quarter of the cores. Off, compiles don't wait |
| Test runs at once (`slots_tests`) | 2 | How many test runs (the run half of cargo test, cargo nextest run) go at once across every pane while Queue heavy work is on, each given the cores shared out. Off, test runs don't wait |
| Package builds at once (`slots_js`) | 2 | How many npm ci, npm install, npm run build, pnpm install and pnpm build go at once across every pane while Queue heavy work is on. Off, they don't wait |

### Diff

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Files in reading order (`diff_order`) | on | M-d lists the changed files flat, documentation first and tests last, with Haiku ordering the code from most abstract to most concrete; off, flat, A to Z |
| Diff told as a story (`diff_story`) | on | M-d's file list grouped into a few pages Haiku names from the whole diff and the commits' messages, each with a heading, two or three sentences that make the case for it to the colleagues reviewing it, and a line of what to watch for, read from the change's core to its edges, and told as a map, in chapters (]c and [c move between them, yS copies the story as Markdown); off, the files keep the reading order by kind. Sends the diff's text and the commit messages, not only the paths, to Claude |
| Diff story progress (`diff_story_progress`) | on | While M-d's story is told, its cover shows what Haiku is reading, each page as Haiku names it and the time taken, t showing Haiku's answer itself as it comes; off, a spinner alone |
| Diff story told ahead (`diff_story_ahead`) | on | When a tab's branch has a new commit, its M-d story is told in the background, so M-d opens on it at once rather than waiting under its cover; sends that branch's diff and commit messages to Claude (Haiku) for each new commit, a run taking up to five and a half minutes |
| Uncommitted changes in M-d (`diff_uncommitted`) | on | M-d's diff runs from the PR's base to the working tree, marking files with changes not yet committed and listing them in a last page; off, it shows the branch's commits only |
| Diff file list wide (`diff_list_wide`) | on | M-d's file list opens wide enough for its longest path (at most half the screen), E narrowing it; off, it opens narrow and E widens it |
| Diff story a page at a time (`diff_story_pages`) | on | ]p and [p in M-d's diff open the story's pages one at a time, each a diff of its own as if it were a commit, [p from the first going back to the whole diff; off, they do nothing |
| Ask Claude about the diff (`diff_ask`) | on | ga in M-d's diff, and M-p's command while the diff is open, ask Haiku a question about the change: it reads the story, the diff and where the cursor is, and may read the repository's files to answer, its answer streamed into a window beside the diff. Sends the diff and the question to Claude |
| Ask in the tab's session (`diff_ask_session`) | on | With Ask Claude about the diff on, ga asks a copy of the tab's own Claude session, which knows the conversation that made the change, on that session's model; the tab's conversation is left as it was, and Haiku answers in its place when the session can't be resumed. Off, it asks a fresh Haiku with the story and the diff alone, which costs less |
| Diff story reads the tab's plan (`diff_story_plan`) | off | M-d's story, told ahead or on opening, is given the tab's plan note (an autopilot item's plan) as well as the commits, so its pages can say what problem each solves and what was passed over; off, it reads the commits and the diff alone. Sends the plan note's words to Claude (Haiku) |
| Tell the diff's story as a map (`diff_story_map`) | on | M-d tells the diff's story as a map: one story in chapters of pages (the goal's chapter, one for each part it can be read without, and the side work), each page with the pages it builds on, as Haiku tells it, and a change shown on every page that can't be read without it. The diff gets a Map mode beside its files (g m and g f, or m), the chapters in a strip across its top, opening in the mode used last; off, the story is one list of pages and there is no Map mode. A story already told is told again the first time either way |
| Diff story notes on changes (`diff_story_notes`) | on | M-d's diff shows Haiku's note over each change worth one, why it is needed, and names the page of a change that belongs to another one than its file; off, only the list's pages |

### Mission control

What belongs to mission control as a whole, then a category for each view, its on/off switch first. In Settings they stand together under a "Mission control" heading, each named `Mission control: <view>` over its options and in a search.

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Click in menus (`menu_mouse`) | on | A click takes an option of mission control's menus, Yes or No of a question or a numbered answer, and a click outside the box closes it; a right click on an item in mission control, the Notifications window or the sidebar opens its menu, and where no item is the view's (the sidebar's own in the sidebar); and a click picks an option of M-m's, the update box's and M-w's menus; off, they take keys alone |
| Notifications hand off to a tab (`notice_to_tab`) | on | Every notification's menu, and the Slack, Inbox, Calendar and Autopilot views' and a failing default branch's in the CI view, offers Send it to a tab, which pastes its words and link into the Claude of a tab you pick and sends them (Paste it into a tab, beside it, without sending), and Open a tab for it, which starts a tab with Claude told about it (a failing default branch's in its repository's main clone, told to fix it). Off, the menus keep their own choices only |
| Send it to a tab submits it (`send_to_tab_submits`) | on | An item's Send it to a tab pastes its words and link into the Claude of the tab you pick and presses Enter, so that Claude starts on it; Paste it into a tab, beside it, pastes them without sending, to add to first. Off, Send it to a tab pastes without sending and Paste it into a tab isn't offered |
| Notifications show in their view (`notice_shows_in_view`) | on | A notification whose kind a view lists (Slack, Inbox, Calendar, PRs, CI, Routines) leads its menu with Show it in the <view> view, which a click or Enter takes: mission control opens on that view with the cursor on its item. Off, its menu starts with its view's own first choice, such as Open it in Slack |
| Open boxes by the click (`boxes_near_click`) | on | A menu or question a click opens appears beside the place clicked, so its options are a short move away; one opened by a key stays in the middle. Off, every box opens in the middle of the screen |
| Tab moves focus (`tab_focus`) | on | Tab and Shift-Tab move a highlight through what can be clicked, as on a webpage, and Enter presses it (Space too, but in the sidebar, where Space peeks): in mission control the views' names, the filter, each section of the view (its rows reached with j and k) and the panes beside it, wrapping round, with [ ] switching views; in the sidebar each of its rows, M-Space opening mission control; and the options of every box and menu; off, Tab switches views in mission control and opens it from the sidebar |
| Show views as the list moves (`mission_nav_follow`) | on | With UX 2.0, moving the cursor through mission control's list of views with j, k or the arrows brings each view to the front as it lands on it, the list keeping the keys; Enter then moves into the view, a view joins back and forward's trail once the cursor rests on it a second, and the list is one Tab stop, Tab going into the view in front and Shift-Tab back. Off, j and k only move the cursor, Enter opens the view, and Tab stops on each view's row |
| Highlight on hover (`hover_highlight`) | on | What a click would press lights under the mouse: in mission control the views' names, the filter, the cards and rows, a menu's options, Yes and No, an answer's options and easy mode's ×; the sidebar's tabs and notifications; the options of M-m's, the update box's and M-w's menus; and the buttons of Notes' toolbar, its boxes' × and M-d's diff. In Ghostty and kitty the mouse pointer also turns into a hand over what a click presses (the workspace's sidebar, header, status line, mission control and boxes) and a resizing arrow over a line between panes. Off, nothing follows the mouse and the pointer is never changed. Takes effect at once in mission control and the sidebar, and when a menu, Notes or the diff next opens |
| Keep the tabs sidebar in mission control (`mission_keep_tabs_sidebar`) | off | With UX 2.0, mission control keeps the tabs sidebar and lists its groups and views in a column of its own beside it, the view to its right; off, while mission control is open the sidebar itself lists its groups and views, the tabs coming back as it closes |
| Pause lookups while away (`pause_when_away`) | on | Stop asking GitHub, Linear and the calendar for mission control's views, and telling the diff's stories ahead, while no terminal is attached, or the computer has been locked, or idle 10 minutes, with no key pressed in Switchboard since (from another computer over SSH too), and catch up on return |
| Look often (`poll_fast`) | off | Ask GitHub and Linear every 15 seconds (the PR board every 30) and look at the Slack Home tab every 10, in place of every one or two minutes: about eight times as many requests, which can reach GitHub's 5,000 an hour |

#### Mission control: Notifications

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Notifications' history (`notification_history`) | on | Mission control's Notifications view keeps every notification of the last two weeks by day, with what each led to and whether the sidebar still shows it, in ~/.local/state/switchboard/notifications on this computer; off, the view shows only the sidebar's notifications, as they are now, and nothing is kept |

#### Mission control: Tabs

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Summaries of each turn (`turn_summaries`) | off | With UX 2.0, each card says what its tab's last turn did, in a sentence Haiku writes as the turn ends from the turn's prompt and last reply, which this sends to Claude; off, as it is by default, nothing is sent and a card shows the reply's first line |

#### Mission control: Calendar

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Calendar (`view_calendar`) | on | Mission control's Calendar view: today from the day /today last saved |
| RSVP from the Calendar (`calendar_rsvp`) | on | Answer an invitation yes, maybe or no from its Enter menu in the Calendar view, through Claude's Google Calendar connector |
| Attendees' photos in the Calendar (`calendar_photos`) | off | Show the photos of who is going to the Calendar view's chosen event, looked up by each attendee's email address in Linear and the Slack relay's app (users:read.email), which sends those addresses to them. Off, as it is by default, no address is sent and each attendee is their initial |

#### Mission control: Reminders

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Reminders (`reminders`) | on | M-p's Remind me about this tab: the tab waits under Reminders with the time left, then moves to Needs you with a banner when it comes due, and mission control's Reminders view shows every tab's reminder, on a month with UX 2.0; off, reminders are neither set nor shown, and the view is gone |

#### Mission control: Slack

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Slack (`view_slack`) | on | Mission control's Slack view: your mentions, DMs, thread replies and chosen channels from the last day, as /slack-updates saved them |
| Slack without a tab (`slack_background`) | on | Mission control looks at Slack itself, as often as "Check Slack for new messages every" says, through your own Slack sign-in while "Read Slack through your own token" is on, else through cheap headless Haiku calls, instead of the Slack routine following the slack-updates skill |
| Check Slack for new messages every (`slack_look_every`) | 30 seconds | How often mission control looks at Slack for new messages while you're at the computer and "Slack without a tab" is on: 15 seconds, 30 seconds, 1, 2 or 5 minutes. A look through Claude's connector rather than your own token goes out at most once a minute, since each is a model call; when Slack answers that its rate limit is spent, the look waits as long as Slack asks and then looks half as often for its next ten looks |
| Slack threads and pictures (`slack_threads`) | on | With UX 2.0, the Slack view shows the chosen message's whole thread beside it, with its pictures and videos, which each look reads through your own Slack sign-in while "Read Slack through your own token" is on, else through Claude's Slack connector (a few more headless Haiku calls a look), and keeps on this computer; off, the view lists the messages alone and reads no threads or files |
| Reply from the Slack view (`slack_reply`) | off | A Slack message's Enter menu in the Slack view offers to reply in its thread or its channel, or in its DM: a box to type in, and Send leaves your words as typed, through Claude's Slack connector, as "Slack messages go out as" says: a draft in Slack for you to send there, or sent at once after asking, naming who can read it. Off, as it is by default since it sends your words to Slack, the menu offers no replies |
| Slack messages go out as (`slack_send_as`) | a draft in Slack | How a message Switchboard sends as you leaves, the Slack view's reply among them: a draft in Slack, made in the conversation's composer through Claude's Slack connector with nothing asked first, the conversation then opened in Slack for you to send it there, since a message the connector sends carries a "Sent using Claude" line that Slack adds and a draft you send carries none; sent at once, after a question naming who can read it, with that line |
| Slack reminders in the Slack view (`slack_reminders`) | on | With a Slack sign-in of your own (switchboard setup slack-user), the Slack view lists your Slack reminders that are due under Reminders, read from Slack every two minutes while you're here, with their notifications among the view's; a reminder's menu marks it complete in Slack after asking. Without a sign-in the view says how to make one. Off, no reminders are read or listed |
| Read Slack through your own token (`slack_token_reads`) | on | With a Slack sign-in of your own (switchboard setup slack-user), the Slack view's look, its threads and pictures, and search everything's Slack results read Slack's Web API with your token rather than a Claude connector call each, falling back to the connector when Slack refuses the token or a scope is missing, and saying why under the view's header. Off, every read goes through the connector |
| Mark what you open read in Slack (`slack_mark_read`) | off | With a Slack sign-in of your own (switchboard setup slack-user), a message whose thread you open in the Slack view (Enter on it, or its thread shown for two seconds) is marked read in Slack up to it, unless you have read further there. Off, as it is by default since it changes what Slack shows you as unread, opening a message marks it seen only here |

#### Mission control: Reopen

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Reopen (`view_reopen`) | on | Mission control's Reopen view: tabs closed and past Claude sessions to resume, with the last screen of each, and the worktrees no tab has open; off, M-o opens it still and nothing else lists them |

#### Mission control: PRs

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| PR board (`pr_board`) | on | Mission control's PRs view, which asks GitHub about your pull requests every two minutes |

#### Mission control: CI

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| CI (`view_ci`) | on | Mission control's CI view: your pull requests failing, running and passing, each failed check with the line of its log that says why and, with UX 2.0, its failing step beside the list, asked of GitHub every two minutes #github |

#### Mission control: Landing

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Landing view (`view_landing`) | on | While a tab is landing or waiting to land, mission control shows each repository's landing queue or merge queue (everyone's PRs in it, asked of GitHub every 15 seconds), in queue order, with the tab that has each; a waiting one's menu moves it or takes it out |

#### Mission control: Autopilot

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Autopilot view (`view_autopilot`) | on | With UX 2.0 on, while autopilot works a note, mission control shows a view of it: the note, its tabs working and open against its slots and cap, today's spend against its limit, and a board of every item it works, a card in the column where it stands (Next up, Blocked while an item waits on another, Working, Waiting on you, Landing, Landed, and Skipped while an item is skipped). Off, its line above the tabs stays and the view goes; on, the view takes the line's place |
| Autopilot (`autopilot`) | on | With it on, M-p's 'Autopilot: work a note' keeps tabs working through a note's checklist, opening the next item with wtn as each lands; the mode itself starts off until you turn it on there. Off, it opens no tabs, and its line in mission control and its notification go |
| Close a worked note's landed tabs (`autopilot_close`) | when seen | When a tab autopilot opened closes once queue-and-clean has landed it and moved it back to the main clone: when seen, as soon as you leave it after seeing it, or 30 minutes after landing if you never look; at once, as soon as it lands; after 5 minutes, or sooner once seen and left; never, left open for you to close. Whatever the choice, a tab in front stays until you leave it and a prompt typed in it after landing keeps it open; one closed without being seen leaves a notification while Notify when autopilot lands is on |
| Autopilot's hands-free mode (`autopilot_handsfree`) | on | Lets autopilot's menu offer 'Hands-free', which has its tabs take the recommended option of what they would ask you (a question box, a question that ends the turn, an item whose plan needs a choice), write each decision into the item's plan as a 'Decided hands-free' line, and land without asking; what your rules or a skill say must be asked still asks. The mode itself starts off. Off, the menu doesn't offer it and every tab asks as usual |
| Autopilot can take GitHub issues (`autopilot_github_issues`) | on | Lets autopilot's settings box offer GitHub issues as a source beside the note: the repository's open issues assigned to you and carrying its label, each opened by you or labeled by someone with write access, read in the background and worked after the note's items, with a comment on the issue when its tab lands or finds it a duplicate, and the issue closed on landing, each asked first unless the tab landed without asking. Nothing is read until you choose the source. Off, the box offers the note alone and autopilot reads no issues |
| Notify when autopilot lands (`autopilot_landing_notices`) | off | Puts each item autopilot lands without asking, and each landed tab it closes before you saw it, in the sidebar's notifications and the Slack relay's main DM. Off, they show only in the Autopilot view's Landed column, the Notifications view's history (as dismissed) and the item's Done: line |
| Estimated finish (`autopilot_eta`) | on | Autopilot's view says when the note's ready items should all be done ("done by about 3:40 PM · 6 left"), from the median time its recent items took from opening to landing, its slots and its tabs still at work, and how many items wait on you (an open choice it won't take, a design board to review). Off, its heading has no estimate |
| Keep opening tabs while I'm away (`autopilot_while_away`) | on | Autopilot keeps opening tabs for the note's next items while you're away (the screen locked, or no input for 10 minutes), up to its slots, most tabs open and spend limit, so it works through the note overnight with a terminal attached. Off, it opens nothing while you're away and finishes only the tabs already open |
| Autopilot's push allowed in auto mode (`autopilot_push_rule`) | on | A tab autopilot opens in a repository that lands straight on a branch starts its Claude with the landing's push command, switchboard land-lock push, allowed for that session, so Claude Code's auto mode doesn't refuse it; the command pushes without asking only while the tab's "Land without asking" is on and its checks pass, and every other push still asks, as does the push after your yes, which no rule allows. Off, the session gets no rule of its own, so auto mode judges the command itself and a tab landing without asking may stop at it |

#### Mission control: Routines

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Routines (`routines`) | on | Prompts of yours on a schedule, each run by a headless Claude allowed only the routine's tools, opening no tab, and the Routines view in mission control; off, no routine runs and the view is gone, the routines kept |

#### Mission control: Tickets

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Ticket board (`ticket_board`) | on | Mission control's Tickets view, which asks Linear about the issues assigned to you every two minutes |

#### Mission control: Inbox

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Inbox (`view_inbox`) | on | Mission control's Inbox view and the sidebar's count of it: your unread Linear mentions, comments and assignments, asked of Linear every two minutes #linear |

#### Mission control: Resources

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Resources (`view_resources`) | on | Mission control's Resources view: the computer's CPU and memory, and a table of the CPU and memory each tab's processes use, sorted by CPU or memory, read from the kernel every few seconds only while the view is in front; with UX 2.0 a board of sparklines and each tab's process tree, whose menu can end a process. Off, the view and its readings are gone |

#### Mission control: Usage

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Usage (`usage`) | on | Mission control's Usage view: today's Claude spend and how much of the rate limits is used |
| Requests in Usage (`request_audit`) | on | The Usage view lists today's requests to services outside this computer (GitHub, Linear, Slack, Anthropic and the rest), by protocol and by service, as Switchboard counts them; off, it leaves them out |

#### Mission control: Keys

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Keys (`view_keys`) | on | Mission control's Keys view, which M-/ opens: every key Switchboard binds, the workspace's grouped by their first tag, then the sidebar's, mission control's, M-d's and Notes', then the commands reached only through M-p, with / to filter, Enter's menu to press a workspace key and m's to find a key by pressing it; with UX 2.0, a board with a heatmap of the Alt keys you pressed this week (counted with habits on); off, M-/ reaches the pane |

### Tabs and status bar

With UX 2.0 on, this category is named "Tabs and header".

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| M-A cycles idle tabs (`idle_walk`) | on | M-A goes to the next idle tab, in the sidebar's Idle order, wrapping at the end, as M-a walks the tabs that need you; off, M-A reaches the pane |
| Tab history (`tab_history`) | on | M-h shows a tab's history: its Claude session on a timeline (its state, your turns, commits, review rounds and context) under its screen as it was at the moment you move to, the screens kept in the server's memory only, never on disk, and the rest read from the session's transcript; off, nothing is kept, M-h says it is off and the header has no History key |
| Widest a lone pane gets (`lone_pane_width`) | off | A tab with one pane (or zoomed into one), and mission control showing one view with no pane beside its list, is drawn at most this wide, against the sidebar, its program given that width and the columns at its right the sidebar's sunken surface with a rule at the pane's right edge; with two or more panes they share the whole width and may be wider, and several views at once fill it too. Besides the widths listed, Columns… takes any number from 80 typed in, as `switchboard setting lone_pane_width <n>` does. A drag of the rule at a lone pane's right edge, or the pane resize keys (C-M-S-h and C-M-S-l), gives that tab alone a width of its own, kept through restarts and cleared with M-p's or the tab's menu's "Use the default width", and the keys in mission control with one view give mission control one, leaving this setting, which every other tab takes, as it is. Off, as it is unless you choose a width, a lone pane fills the width |
| Hints beside a narrow pane (`spare_hints`) | on | The columns right of a lone pane narrowed by Widest a lone pane gets list, centered in them, what the pane can open, each its key and a click that does what the key does: the tab's note, a split to the right, the PR and the ticket when the tab has one (in Claude's place with UX 2.0 on), and a link read beside the pane, with how to widen the pane under them; nothing shows where they don't fit. Off, those columns are the plain sunken surface |
| Open views beside a narrow pane (`views_beside_narrow_pane`) | on | With a tab's one pane narrowed by Widest a lone pane gets and at least 80 columns spare at its right, M-d's diff, lazygit, and the PR and ticket views that open in Claude's place open in those columns instead, the pane keeping its width beside them (the diff takes Diff's share beside a narrow pane, narrowing the pane while it is open); the same key, q or M-w closes the view and the tab is one narrow pane again. With fewer columns spare, or something already beside the pane, they open in its place. Off, they always open in the pane's place |
| Diff's share beside a narrow pane (`diff_beside_share`) | 75% | How much of the width beside the sidebar M-d's diff takes when it opens beside a narrow lone pane: half, two thirds or three quarters, the pane narrowed to the rest while the diff is open and given its own width back when it closes, or the spare columns alone, the pane keeping its width. Where the spare columns already give the diff more, it takes them. Dragging the line between them resizes for that diff alone |
| Open boxes beside a narrow pane (`boxes_beside_narrow_pane`) | on | With a tab's one pane narrowed by Widest a lone pane gets, a box about the tab (its links, pictures and the tabs it mentions, answering its Claude, which note is its, naming or renaming it, its menu and its full title, M-m's PR readiness, sending its Claude's last reply on, Work on this item) opens as a pane in the empty columns at the pane's right, as tall as they are, the hints there hidden while it is open; every other box, a message and a notification among them, goes in the middle of the screen. With too few columns spare it goes in the middle of the screen too. Off, every box goes in the middle of the screen |
| Show images beside a narrow pane (`images_beside_pane`) | on | With a tab's one pane narrowed by Widest a lone pane gets, a picture Claude or a skill shows you (`switchboard look`) is drawn as a pane in the empty columns at the pane's right, two or more stacked top to bottom with their names, the hints there hidden while they show; M-Esc or a click on them closes them, and with more than fit the wheel over them and M-PgDn and M-PgUp page through them. With too few columns spare, a split, another tab in front, more than six pictures or a terminal that draws no images, they open in the desktop's viewer, as a video or a PDF always does. Off, they always open in the desktop's viewer |
| Tab age and stage times (`tab_age`) | on | Shows how long each tab has been open, as 'opened 1h12m' in the UX 2.0 tab header and on mission control's cards, and how long each stage of an autopilot tab took (or has taken so far) on its board; off, none of them is shown, while a landed item's Done line still says how long it took, since the landing writes it |
| Search what panes show (`tab_search_panes`) | on | M-j's picker (and M-b w) finds a tab by what its panes show as well as by its name: each pane's screen and the last lines of its scrollback (Lines searched in each pane) are read in the server's memory as you type, never written anywhere, and each tab found that way shows its matching lines under its row, the tabs found by name first; its row's menu can go to the match in copy mode. Search everything (M-S-f) finds tabs by what their panes show the same way. Off, the picker and search everything match the tabs' names alone |
| Lines searched in each pane (`tab_search_lines`) | 1000 lines | How far back Search what panes show reads each pane's scrollback, above its screen: 200 lines, 1000, or all the pane keeps |
| M-{ and M-} go back and forward (`tab_back_forward`) | on | M-{ and M-} go back and forward through the tabs and mission control views you visited, as a browser's back and forward do, and do nothing with nowhere further to go, the ← and → buttons before mission control's ⊞ do the same, and M-Tab goes to the tab or view before; off, they go to the tab on the left and right, as the prefix's p and n always do, the buttons are left out, and M-Tab goes to the tab before |
| One Claude to a tab (`one_claude_per_tab`) | on | A Claude session started in a tab already running one moves to a tab of its own, since the sidebar, the Slack relay, M-c and M-m each take a tab's Claude to be one pane |
| Name new tabs (`name_new_tabs`) | on | M-t and M-n ask what to call the tab they open: a name typed in, its ticket and the ticket's title, or shaping a ticket, each optional; off, the tab is named after its directory |
| Show a tab's full title (`tab_title`) | on | M-p's "Show this tab's full title", the sidebar's i and a tab's menu in mission control (and M-T, with "M-T reopens the last closed tab" off) show the tab's whole title in a box, with its branch, directory, PR and ticket, which the sidebar and the status line cut; off, M-T reaches the pane |
| M-T reopens the last closed tab (`reopen_tab_key`) | on | M-T reopens the tab closed most recently, in its directory with its Claude resumed; pressed again, the one before. Off, M-T shows the tab's full title as before |
| Messages in a box (`message_boxes`) | on | What a key or script has to say shows in a small box in the middle of the screen, which any key closes, rather than on the status line for a moment |
| Opening-link message (`link_opening_message`) | on | Opening a link says where it is going (the site, Chrome and its profile) in a box until the browser comes to the front, since a profile's Chrome can take seconds to start. Off, links open with nothing said |
| Second status line (`second_status`) | off | The line under the tabs with the tab's name, branch, PR and Claude session |
| PRs and tickets in tab names (`tab_lookups`) | on | Look up each tab's PR number on GitHub and its ticket's title on Linear, in the background |
| Project colors (`accent_colors`) | on | A color of its own for each project's tab, made from its directory; off, every tab is gray |
| Tab colors (`tab_colors`) | soft | The colors tabs take from their project, in the tab bar, the sidebar, the header and Claude's statusline: soft pastels, as the UX 2.0 canvas draws them, or the stronger colors from before. Each tab keeps its own hue either way |
| A color a tab (`tab_colors_apart`) | on | Each tab gets the hue farthest from the open tabs' as it opens, a deeper shade of it when it is close to one, kept while it is open and through restarts, so tabs, and the Usage view's spend chart, tell apart. Off, a tab wears its directory's color, the same for every tab of one checkout |
| Folder for new tabs instead of home (`new_tab_home`) | Home | A new tab, or a split, that would start in your home folder starts in this folder instead, as does the first tab when the workspace starts, while it is a folder: Claude Code asks whether to trust the home folder every time a session starts there, and a folder here is asked about once. A tab opened from a pane in another folder keeps that folder, and a session reopened or resumed keeps the folder it started in. Its menu's Folder… takes a folder typed in, `~/...` or absolute, refusing one that isn't there, as `switchboard setting new_tab_home <folder>` does. Home, the default, starts them at home |
| Health light (`health_light`) | on | A red mark in the status bar when saves, the sidebar or the Slack relay quietly stop |

### Notes

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| A Notes app in each tab (`notes_per_tab`) | on | M-e opens a Notes app beside the pane in the tab you're in, on that tab's note, and every other tab keeps its own open; a note open in one tab shows read-only in another, naming the tab that has it, until that tab's app lets it go. Off, there is one Notes, which M-e moves between the tabs |
| Hide the sidebar beside a tab (`notes_hide_sidebar`) | on | When M-e brings Notes beside another tab's pane, put the notes app's sidebar away so the note has the room, and show it again in Notes' own tab |
| Notes command palette (`notes_palette`) | on | M-p with Notes in front lists every command Notes has, each with its key, ahead of the workspace's, as Ctrl-Shift-P does in Notes run alone; off, M-p leaves them out and Ctrl-Shift-P in Notes run alone says the palette is off |
| Ctrl-V pastes an image (`notes_ctrl_v_image`) | on | Ctrl-V while typing in a note pastes the clipboard's image under the line, as in Claude Code, when the clipboard holds one; off, or with no image there, Ctrl-V types the next key as it is, as in Vim |
| Images drawn in a note (`notes_images`) | on | An image pasted into a note (Format menu, Paste image) drawn under the line that names it, in a terminal that speaks the kitty graphics protocol (Ghostty and kitty do), or one that gets sixel by Image protocol (Windows Terminal, by default); off, it is its name, which Enter opens as "Enter shows a picture full size" says |
| Draw Mermaid diagrams (`notes_mermaid`) | on | A ```mermaid block in a note (a flowchart, its subgraphs among it, or a sequence diagram) drawn as its diagram while the cursor is outside it, and as its code while the cursor is in it; the Format menu's Show diagram code shows a note's blocks as code until Show diagrams. Off, every block shows as its code |
| Recent notes in the sidebar (`notes_recent`) | on | Notes' sidebar shows the notes you opened last under Recent, above the folders. Off, the sidebar shows only the folders. |
| Relative line numbers (`notes_relative_numbers`) | off | Notes numbers each line by its distance from the cursor's line, the cursor's line showing its own number, as Vim's relativenumber, for jumps like 5j. Off, every line shows its own number. |
| Chips on checklist items (`notes_item_chips`) | on | With UX 2.0 on, each checklist item shows at its right the tab working on it (its number in the tab's color, a click going there), "landed <hash>" once ticked with its commit named under it, "plan" where it links its plan (a click opening it) and "needs a choice" while one waits, its text cut with … before them except on the cursor's line; off, items are their text alone |
| Enter shows a picture full size (`picture_full_size`) | on | Enter or a click on a note's picture shows it full size over the notes pane, as it does a picture or diagram under the cursor in the PR, ticket, link and file views, drawn by the workspace in a terminal that speaks the kitty graphics protocol (Ghostty, kitty), or as sixel where Image protocol gives a terminal sixel (Windows Terminal, by default), with "Images in the workspace" on, Esc going back; off, or where the terminal can't, it opens in Quick Look or the desktop's image viewer |
| Toolbar (`notes_toolbar`) | on | A row of buttons over each note, one for each of the Format menu's styles (bold, italic, headings, lists, checklist, table, link, image) and ⋯ for the whole menu, each acting on the line or the selection; off, the Format menu is Ctrl-P alone |
| Tell a note's new tab its note (`note_tab_prompt`) | on | A new tab opened for a note from Notes starts Claude knowing the note's title and file (added to its system prompt, no turn spent), to read when you ask, or first while "Point Claude at the tab's note" is on; off, it starts as any other tab |
| Notes beside a note's tab (`note_tab_beside`) | on | Opening a note's tab from Notes (t, or M-p's Open the tab for the note) moves Notes beside the tab's Claude showing the note, as M-e does; off, it only goes to the tab |
| Show a note's item whole in wtn's list (`wtn_item_preview`) | on | M-p's "Start work on a note's checklist items" shows the item under the cursor whole in the lower half of its list, its full text and what is indented under it, since the list cuts each item to one line; off, the list has the whole box |
| M-e opens the tab's plan or autopilot's note (`note_by_precedence`) | on | M-e shows the note you linked to the tab, else the plan of an autopilot tab's item, else, on an autopilot tab, the note autopilot is working through, else the note shown there last. Off, M-e shows the tab's note or the one shown there last, as before. |
| Ask for a tab's note (`ask_tab_note`) | on | When M-e brings Notes beside a tab with no note of its own yet, ask which note is the tab's, a few likely ones and then every note newest first, and show that note beside it from then on |
| Point Claude at the tab's note (`tab_note_context`) | off | As a Claude session starts in a tab with a note of its own, tell it the note's path and to read it before its first reply, so the note's standing rules reach every session in the tab, in any repository, while Switchboard loads as a plugin (claude_plugin), whose session-start hook does it; off, a session reads the note only when asked to |

### Appearance

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Images in the workspace (`server_images`) | on | Draws avatars, pictures, meters, charts and the shadow under boxes as images where the terminal can show them: by kitty's graphics protocol (Ghostty, kitty), or as sixel where Image protocol gives a terminal sixel (Windows Terminal, by default), the shadow there staying half blocks, since a sixel can't sit under text. Off, they are drawn with characters |
| Scroll bars (`scroll_bars`) | on | A scroll bar at the right of every list, view and pane with more than fits, showing where you are: a click on its track pages toward the click, and dragging its thumb scrolls with it. Off, nothing is drawn there |
| People's photos (`photos`) | on | Avatars show people's photos from Slack (through the Slack relay's app, with the users:read scope), Linear and GitHub, and, with calendar_photos on, calendar attendees' by their address, each fetched once and kept in ~/.cache/switchboard/photos for 30 days. Off, nothing is fetched and an avatar is the person's initial |
| A cursor in the views (`view_cursor`) | on | Gives the PR, ticket, link and file views a cursor, a yellow › that j and k move from line to line, a picture or diagram one step, the screen scrolling to keep it in sight; Enter or a click on a picture or diagram shows it full size over the view, as a note's does, Esc going back. Off, the views scroll as less does, and a click on a picture opens its link |
| Pictures and diagrams in the views (`view_images`) | on | Draws the pictures on lines of their own and the Mermaid diagrams in the PR, ticket, link and file views as images where the terminal can show them (Ghostty, kitty, or as sixel where Image protocol gives a terminal sixel, Windows Terminal by default), fetching the pictures and drawing the diagrams with Mermaid in Chrome, and draws the diagrams of the description beside mission control's PR and ticket boards the same way (with "Images in the workspace" on too). Off, a picture is its name and a diagram is drawn with characters |
| UX 2.0 (`ux2`) | off | No status line: each tab has a header naming it, with the keys of what it can show in Claude's place (M-c Claude, M-d Diff, M-v PR, M-i Ticket, M-e Note); the sidebar starts with its notifications; M-v and M-i show the PR and the ticket in Claude's place rather than beside it, laid out as their pages are with a panel of their facts beside the description; and M-d's diff reads as one scroll of files under its story's chapters, the chapter's why over the diff and each hunk's note in a card under it ([UX 2.0](#ux-20)). Off, the status line, the sidebar and those keys are as before |
| Theme (`theme`) | dark | The status line's, the pane borders' and the boxes' colors, dark drawn in the UX 2.0 canvas's colors on a terminal that shows truecolor (Windows Terminal among them) and in the 256-color palette elsewhere; system follows the terminal's own background, which it is asked for as it attaches |
| Set the terminal's colors (`terminal_colors`) | off | While Switchboard is attached, sets your terminal's background and text colors to the dark theme's, so the padding around the window matches; puts the terminal's own back when you detach. Off, the terminal keeps its own colors. |
| Image protocol (`image_protocol`) | by the terminal | How pictures (a note's images, the PR and ticket image views, the pictures and diagrams in the PR, ticket, link and file views, and the workspace's own: avatars, meters, charts, switches, the diagrams beside a board, a picture shown full size), and the rounded ends of its pills, reach your terminal: auto picks by the terminal, kitty's graphics protocol for Ghostty and kitty, sixel for one that says it draws sixel when asked as it attaches (Windows Terminal from 1.22, foot, WezTerm, xterm with sixel, mlterm), and the text forms for any other; kitty sends them by kitty's protocol to every terminal, as chosen, one that doesn't speak it showing boxes in their place; sixel has the workspace draw each picture itself as sixel where it shows for every terminal, as chosen, Ghostty and kitty included, one that doesn't draw sixel showing blanks in their place, the shadow under a box drawn with characters. Notes, the image view and the PR, ticket, link and file views opened after a change draw by it ([Pictures in Windows Terminal](#pictures-in-windows-terminal)) |

### Safety

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Rules in reviews (`review_rules`) | on | codex-review gives the reviewer the repository's CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md as the base has them and your own ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md with what it imports, and reports a change that breaks one; off, it reviews without them, and your rules aren't sent to the reviewer |
| Review before pushing (`review_before_push`) | on | In this repository, a push to main needs a clean /review-loop first (.githooks/pre-push); off, it pushes without one |
| Security review in the review loop (`review_security`) | on | /review-loop runs Claude Code's /security-review on the branch once before its first round, its findings fixed or asked about as the rounds' are |
| M-w closes the tab from Claude or a lone pane (`close_tab_from_claude`) | on | M-w in a tab's Claude pane, in a tab's one pane, or on its sidebar row, asks to close the tab, and anywhere else closes only the pane; off, M-w closes the pane it is pressed in and the last pane closes its tab |
| Ask before closing a pane (`confirm_close`) | on | M-w asks first when a program runs in the pane it closes (a shell at its prompt closes at once), and before closing a tab from its Claude pane, its one pane or its sidebar row; off, it closes the pane or tab at once whatever runs in it |

### Palette

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Learn from what you pick (`palette_learn`) | on | M-p remembers what you typed before each pick, and puts that command first when you type the same again |
| Pictures and videos Claude named (`conversation_media`) | on | M-p's "Open an image or video from this pane" also lists the files the last turns of the tab's Claude named that the screen doesn't show, read from its transcript. Off, it lists only what the pane and the tab's note show |
| Links you gave Claude (`prompt_links`) | on | M-p's link commands also list the links in what you sent the pane's Claude, pasted text included, which Claude Code doesn't show. Off, they list only what the pane shows |
| Pane commands read the tab's note (`pane_commands_read_note`) | on | M-p's link, picture and mentioned-tab commands also read the note open in the tab (M-e): from Claude's pane its links, files and tabs after the pane's, and from the notes pane the tab's other panes' after the note's. Off, each reads only the pane it runs from |
| Commands button (`palette_button`) | on | A ⌘ Commands button that opens M-p when clicked: in the status bar after the session name, or with UX 2.0 at the right end of the tab header's keys, where a click while the palette is open closes it |

### Help

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Tips (`tips`) | on | A box in the top-right corner, once each, when a key would help (M-a, M-j, putting a pane back); any key closes it |
| Suggestions from how you work (`habits`) | on | Count the keys you press, the commands you pick in M-p and the mission control views you open, on this computer only and never sent anywhere, and suggest in the notifications a view to turn off that you never open, a key for a command you keep picking in M-p, or a feature that is off which what you do would use; off, what was counted is removed |
| Easy mode (`easy_keys`) | off | Keys for people who don't know Vim's, in Notes and M-d's diff: Shift-arrows and the mouse select, typing replaces a selection, Cmd-S saves, Cmd-Z undoes, Cmd-Shift-Z redoes and Cmd-X cuts in a note (where the terminal sends them as the guide says), and the diff gets a row of buttons for its keys; mission control's boxes, M-m's, the update box's and M-w's menus, messages, tips, and Notes' Format menu, palette and finder get a × in their top-right corner that a click or Tab and Enter closes, with Click in menus on (lists to pick from, such as M-p's, keep Esc alone); Vim's keys keep working. Takes effect when Notes or the diff next opens |
| Key help line (`key_help_line`) | on | With Easy mode on, a line at the bottom of Notes and M-d's diff naming the keys that work there |
| Alt-arrows move between panes (`pane_arrows`) | on | M-Left, M-Right, M-Up and M-Down move to the pane on that side, as C-w h, l, k and j do in Vim; off, they reach the pane |
| Resize the pane beside a board (`resize_side_panes`) | on | In mission control, the pane resize keys (C-M-S-h and C-M-S-l) and a drag of its left edge widen or narrow the pane beside the PRs, Tickets and Autopilot boards, each board keeping its width. Off, the pane keeps its share of the screen and the keys and its edge do nothing there |
| C-w then h, j, k or l moves between panes (`ctrl_w_panes`) | on | C-w followed by h, j, k or l moves to the pane on that side, as in Vim, which itself still gets C-w; C-w twice sends one to the pane. In mission control, C-w and h or l move between a view's side panel, list and the pane beside it, and C-w and j or k to the next or last section a view stacks in its list. Off, C-w reaches the pane as it is, deleting a word in a shell or Claude's prompt, and a mission control view's list keeps the keys |
| Extended keys (`extended_keys`) | on | Your terminal is asked to tell keys such as Ctrl-Shift-P from Ctrl-P and Shift-Enter from Enter, and a program that asks for them (Notes, Neovim) gets them so, while the rest get the keys as before; off, every key reaches every program as a plain terminal sends it. Takes effect when a terminal next attaches and for panes opened after |

### Updates

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Update check (`update_check`) | open, hourly | Offer a new release of Switchboard: when a terminal attaches and every hour, only when one attaches, or never |
| Offer to restart after an update (`upgrade_after_update`) | on | Once switchboard update has put a new release in place, ask whether to restart the workspace on it now, keeping every tab and what runs in it; off, it only says how (switchboard upgrade) |
| Your files from a git repository (`files_from_repo`) | off | Your own files (settings.json, keys.json, sidebar.json and the rest of ~/.config/switchboard) are read from and written to a clone of the git repository files.json names (Settings' Your files' repository), in ~/.local/share/switchboard/files: pulled as Switchboard starts and every ten minutes, and each change committed and pushed at once, after leak-check has read it and asked about anything it found. files.json, trusted-dirs, repos.json, sidebar-plugins, slack-relay.json, linear.json, oncall.json, oncall-rules.md, slack-updates.json, secrets.json and leak-check.txt stay on this computer, as this setting does. Off, files.json is ignored and your files are the ones in ~/.config/switchboard |
| Skills and instructions from the clone (`assets_from_checkout`) | on | A build made from a clone reads its skills, Claude settings, instructions and settings list from that clone's assets/ folder, so a change there is live once it lands: the plugin and the parts you set up are brought up to date within seconds, with no rebuild or restart. A release build has no clone and keeps what it carries. Off, every build uses what it carries |
| Build before restarting (`build_before_restart`) | on | M-p's "Restart Switchboard on the latest main" first brings the main clone of the checkout a build from a clone came from up to date with origin, on its default branch with nothing uncommitted, and builds it (cargo build --release --locked; a try builds its worktree) and puts it in place of the binary the server runs, restarting only when that is new (from a cargo target directory, which other worktrees write over, the build goes to ~/.local/bin/switchboard, else a copy in ~/.cache/switchboard/build, and the restart runs from there); off, it restarts on the binary on disk as it is, when that is new |
| Say when main is ahead of this build (`build_behind_notice`) | on | When Switchboard's main clone has commits on its default branch (as origin last answered) that the running build lacks, a notification says how many landings the build lacks, counted as the server starts, after each landing and every hour, and its menu restarts on the latest main; off, nothing is counted or shown |

### Slack relay

| Setting | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Slack relay (`view_relay`) | on | Mission control's Slack relay view: whether the relay is running, what it sent today, and which kinds of message reach you and how, with presets |
| Close the relay's tab when it stops (`relay_close_tab`) | on | Stopping the Slack relay, from M-p, `slack-relay stop`, `stop` in its DM or Ctrl-C in its tab, closes its tab; a crash or a start it refuses keeps the tab at a shell with the error. Off, the tab stays at a shell whenever the relay ends |
| Prompts and questions (`relay_send_prompts`) | Notify me | Where a prompt, question or wait goes: Notify me (its thread, with a line there mentioning you) or Thread only |
| Dialogs before a session starts (`relay_send_dialogs`) | Notify me | Where a dialog before a session starts goes: Notify me or Thread only |
| Finished turns (`relay_send_finished`) | Thread only | Where each turn's last message goes: Notify me, Thread only or Don't send |
| Turns another tab or a task started (`relay_send_other_turns`) | off | Posts a finished turn that another tab's message or a background task started, or that wrote no reply, as Finished turns says; off, only turns you started that wrote a reply are posted |
| Tabs that may be stuck (`relay_send_stuck`) | Notify me | Where a note that a tab may be stuck goes: Notify me, Thread only or Don't send |
| Commits, pushes and pull requests (`relay_send_milestones`) | Thread only | Where each commit, push and pull request goes: Notify me, Thread only or Don't send |
| Failed commits, pushes and pull requests (`relay_send_failures`) | Notify me | Where a failed one goes: Notify me, Thread only or Don't send |
| Full context (`relay_send_context`) | Thread only | Where a tab past 90% of its context is said: Notify me, Thread only or Don't send |
| Notices from other programs (`relay_send_notices`) | Notify me | What `slack-relay notify` sends: Notify me, or Don't send, which tells the sender it wasn't sent |
| Autopilot's tabs (`relay_send_autopilot`) | Like any tab | Like any tab, or Landings and failures only: a tab autopilot opened gets only its prompts, dialogs and failures |
| Quiet hours (`relay_quiet_hours`) | off | Between Quiet hours from and to (`relay_quiet_from`, `relay_quiet_to`, 10pm and 7am), what would notify goes to its thread only, but for prompts and dialogs while Prompts notify in quiet hours (`relay_quiet_prompts`, on) is on |
| Haiku summaries (`haiku_summaries`) | on | One line on what each tab is doing in the relay's Home tab and progress, each a paid Haiku call |

## Commands for scripts

Scripts, hooks and skills act on the running workspace through `switchboard <command>`, much as they once called tmux. These commands never start a server; with none running, they fail. Each acts on a target given as `-t %<pane>` or `-t @<tab>` (as `switchboard list` and `switchboard tabs` show them); without one, on the pane the command runs in (its `SWITCHBOARD_PANE`), else the pane in front on the terminal used last. A tab given where a pane is wanted means its active pane, and a pane given where a tab is wanted, or for a tab's option with `show` and `set` (`@tab_label`, `@tab_name` and the other `@tab_` ones, autopilot's `@autopilot` ones, and `@agent`), means its tab. A directory given with `-c` is read from where the command runs, `~` included.

```text
switchboard list                         sessions, tabs and panes, as JSON;
                                         each tab's `stage` is the sidebar's
                                         group for it (key, title, the
                                         group's rank, its place in it)
switchboard new-tab [-s session] [-c dir] [-n name] [-d] [-E] [-- command...]
                                         prints the new tab's and pane's ids;
                                         -d leaves the current tab in front,
                                         -E puts the tab at the end
switchboard split [-t pane] [-L|-R|-U|-D] [-c dir] [-p percent] [-d] [-- command...]
                                         a new pane on that side (right when
                                         none is given); prints its id
switchboard respawn-pane [-t pane] [-c dir] [-k] [-- command...]
                                         a new program in the same pane;
                                         -k ends one still running
switchboard send-keys [-t pane] [-l|-p] key...
                                         keys by name (Enter, C-c, M-x), a word
                                         that isn't one typed as text; -l types
                                         them all as text, -p pastes them
switchboard capture-pane [-t pane] [-S lines] [-D]
                                         the pane's screen, with that many lines
                                         of history above it; -D leaves what is
```
