```text
                                         drawn dimmed blank, as hints are
switchboard select-pane [-t pane] [-L|-R|-U|-D]
                                         focus a pane, or the one beside it on
                                         that side (the sidebar past the left
                                         edge), as the diff and Notes ask at
                                         their edges
switchboard select-tab -t tab            bring a tab to the front
switchboard switch-client -t tab         bring a tab to the front and show it
                                         in the terminal you used last, moving
                                         it to the tab's session
switchboard kill-pane [-t pane]          close a pane, without asking
switchboard kill-tab [-t tab]            close a tab, without asking
switchboard rename-tab [-t tab] name     name a tab; "" names it after its
                                         directory again
switchboard set [-t target] [-u] @option [value]
                                         set (or with -u, unset) a tab's or
                                         pane's option
switchboard show [-t target] @option     print an option's value
switchboard display [-t pane] pane|tab|session|cwd|command|title
                                         print one thing about a pane
switchboard say [-t seconds] text...     a message in a box
switchboard picture [-t pane] <file>     show a picture full size over the
                                         pane, Esc going back; fails where
                                         the terminal draws no images
switchboard look [-t pane] <file>...     show pictures beside the tab's narrow
                                         lone pane, stacked when several; in
                                         the desktop's viewer with no room
                                         there, and a video or a PDF always
switchboard seen [-t tab]                mark the tab seen, as looking at it
                                         does: a finished Claude and a
                                         reminder come due
switchboard tip <name> | tip seen <name> | tip reset
                                         show a tip once, mark it shown, or
                                         show every tip again
switchboard press <action> [arg]         run an action as its key would, by its
                                         name in keys.json (only note-tab and
                                         attach-note take an arg, the note;
                                         work-on-item, what
                                         Notes' gw presses, takes the note and
                                         the item's line; plan-item, M-Enter
                                         in Notes' Add an item, the note and
                                         the item)
switchboard sidebar-repo [-s session] <dir> | --all
                                         filter the sidebar to the repository
                                         dir is in, or clear the filter
switchboard autopilot-next <note> <line> put the unticked item on that line of
                                         the note autopilot works next in its
                                         queue
switchboard autopilot-start [--change] <fields>
                                         start autopilot, while off, with the
                                         settings given as JSON, or with
                                         --change, while on, change them (the
                                         Slack relay's Autopilot form;
                                         refused run by hand)
switchboard autopilot-board              autopilot's progress as JSON, for the
                                         Slack relay's Home tab
switchboard autopilot-pause on|off       pause or resume autopilot
switchboard sources [refresh [view]]     mission control's GitHub and Linear
                                         data and today's calendar as JSON,
                                         or looked up again now
switchboard list-keys                    every key and what it does
switchboard server-version               the build the server runs, as
                                         switchboard --version names it
switchboard save                         save the layout now; prints its file
switchboard restore-layout [<file>]      bring back the tabs from before the last
                                         start (layout.json.start), or of a save,
                                         beside the workspace's one tab
switchboard detach [-s session]          detach every terminal, or a session's
switchboard kill-server                  save, then end the server and its panes
switchboard upgrade                      restart the server on the build installed
                                         now, keeping every pane, its program and
                                         the terminals attached
```

`set` and `show` without `-t` put an option whose name starts with `@tab_` or `@autopilot`, or is `@agent`, on the caller's tab, and any other on its pane. A few are the workspace's own: `@tab_name` names the tab; `show` gives `@tab_label`, the name the tab bar shows, PR number included, `@tab_path`, the directory it is named after, and `@agent`, the tab's most urgent state. On a pane, `@agent_state` and `@agent_since` are Claude's state there and since when (seconds), `@agent_activity` its tool call in a few words, `@agent_wait` what it waits on, `@agent_loop` the loop it runs (review, ci, queue or deploy) and `@agent_phase` the step a loop says it is at, which `land-lock` sets as it lands on the default branch.

Typed by hand in a pane, by you in a shell or by Claude, which may run `switchboard set` and `switchboard respawn` without asking, `set` and `respawn-pane` stay in the pane's own tab: `set` names another tab only to set its `@tab_name`, and sets none of Switchboard's own marks (autopilot's `@autopilot` ones but `@autopilot_pushed`, which queue-and-clean sets once it has pushed, and `@allow_passthrough` and `@notes_pane`, which let a pane's pictures reach the terminal), and `respawn-pane` starts no program of its own there, only the shell. The workspace says why in a box when it refuses one. Switchboard's own programs (Notes, the worktree commands, the landing lock, the Slack relay) aren't held to it.

```sh
# A tab in the background for a build, its pane's id kept for later
read tab pane < <(switchboard new-tab -d -n build -c ~/code/app)
switchboard send-keys -t "$pane" 'make test' Enter
switchboard capture-pane -t "$pane" -S 200
```

A pane closes when its program ends, so a command given to `new-tab` or `split` takes its pane with it when it finishes; type it into the shell, as above, to keep what it printed. Each of these says how it is used with `--help` after its name.

Six more commands are programs of their own:

- `switchboard tabs` prints a line per pane of every tab, tab-separated: the tab's number, the pane's place (`main:@3.%12`), the tab's name as the tab bar shows it, Claude's state there (blank when idle), the program in front, and the directory; then, where the pane runs Claude, its session ID, its process ID, the name its row in Claude Code's `ListAgents` shows, and its address (`uds:` and its messaging socket), which `SendMessage` takes as its `to`, blank where it runs none. Claude reads it to see what the other tabs are doing and to reach another tab's Claude. A tab is named by that first number, never by the digits that end a `ListAgents` name (`switchboard-66`), and a session finds its own tab as the line whose place ends in its `SWITCHBOARD_PANE` (with the plugin on, `switchboard claude-context` tells each session its tab's number and label as it starts), so a message it sends says which tab it comes from. `--json` prints the same as an array of objects (`tab`, `pane`, `name`, `state`, `program`, `dir`, `session`, `pid`, `agent`, `address`, a blank one null), for scripts.
- `switchboard respawn [-t pane] [-c dir] <command> [args...]` replaces a pane's program with another in the same pane, which keeps its id, place and tab, and leaves a shell there when it ends. `wt`, `wtn`, `wtb` and `wtpr` run as the worktree commands' shell functions. The arguments are words of their own, never read by a shell. Skills use it to hand a tab over to the work it opens. Run in a pane, it respawns only a pane of its own tab, as `wt`, `wtn`, `wtb` or `claude --resume <session>`, and refuses anything else, saying why.
- `switchboard landing [<dir>]` says how a repository lands its work, through a PR or straight on its default branch, and `switchboard apply-actions <repo> <from> <to>` prints what a landed change needs to take effect, and `switchboard apply install <repo>` or `switchboard apply relay` does Switchboard's part of it ("Landing: `/queue-and-clean`").
- `switchboard setting <key>` prints one of Settings' values as it stands, `on` or `off`, or the choice (`switchboard setting update_check` prints `hourly`); `switchboard setting <key> <value>` changes it as Settings would, and `switchboard setting <key> --reset` gives it its default back.
- `switchboard headless [--size <cols>x<rows>] [--session <name>] [--term <name>]` attaches a client with no terminal and prints its id, with no `--size` at the narrowest size on which no mission control view stacks its panes and no box drops its extras (235x50 with the default sidebar; `switchboard headless --min-size` prints it); `switchboard headless-keys <id> <key>...` types keys by name into it (`M-Space`, `]`, `j`, `Enter`) as a terminal sends them, and `switchboard headless-screen <id>` prints its whole screen, the panes, the sidebar, mission control and the boxes over them, as text, `--ansi` with its colors, or `--png <file>` as a picture drawn by headless Chrome, as Ghostty draws it, with the server's images. It attaches as Ghostty (`xterm-ghostty`, saying it draws truecolor), so the server draws for it what it draws for Ghostty; `--term <name>` names another terminal (`--term xterm-256color` for the 256 colors and text forms a plain terminal gets). `switchboard headless --stop <id>` detaches it. It reaches the server the other commands do, so run it with a demo's or a try's prefix; the workspace you are in is refused unless `--yes` is given, since a second client there takes its screen's size and sees what you see.
- `switchboard platform <what>` does what differs between macOS and Linux, for shell scripts: `name`, `secret <service> [-a account]`, `secret-set <service> [-a account]` (the secret read from stdin), `copy` (stdin to the clipboard), `paste`, `notify <title> <text>`, `presence` (idle milliseconds and whether the screen is locked), `open <file or link>`, `front-app` and `chrome-local-state`.

`switchboard help` lists every command, and `switchboard --version` prints the version and the commit it was built from.
